=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N squares arranged in a row, labeled 1, 2, \ldots, N from left to right.
You are given M pairs of integers (L_1, R_1), \ldots, (L_M, R_M).
A square j is defined to be bad if and only if there exists some i such that L_i \leq j \leq R_i.
Determine whether you can move from square 1 to square N by repeatedly performing the following action:

- Let your current square be x. Choose an integer i that satisfies all of the following conditions, and move to square x + i.
- A \leq i \leq B
- x + i \leq N
- Square x + i is not bad.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M A B
L_1 R_1
L_2 R_2
\vdots
L_M R_M

Output

If it is possible to reach square N by repeating the action described in the problem statement, print Yes. Otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 10^{12}
- 0 \leq M \leq 2 \times 10^4
- 1 \leq A \leq B \leq 20
- 1 < L_i \leq R_i < N \ (1 \leq i \leq M)
- R_i < L_{i+1} \ (1 \leq i \leq M - 1)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

24 2 3 5
7 8
17 20

Sample Output 1

Yes

You can move to square N in this way: 1 \to 6 \to 9 \to 12 \to 16 \to 21 \to 24.

Sample Input 2

30 1 5 8
4 24

Sample Output 2

No

Sample Input 3

100 4 10 11
16 18
39 42
50 55
93 99

Sample Output 3

Yes

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Therefore, we can model each safe segment by:

- Exit positions: reachable positions in the last B positions of the segment (from which jumps can leave to later segments).

Now, semigroup membership function:

Now, how to precompute semigroup dp for A<B. We can implement:
def make_semigroup(A,B):
   if A==B:
       return lambda n: n>=0 and n % A ==0
   threshold = A*(A-1)
   maxn = threshold + B maybe? We only need dp for n < threshold. But in semigroup check, if n >= threshold return True. For n < threshold, dp[n].
   dp = [False]*(threshold) maybe if threshold=0 handle. dp[0]=True.
   for n in range(1, threshold):
       for d in range(A, B+1):
           if d <= n and dp[n-d]:
              dp[n]=True; break
   return lambda n: n>=0 and (n >= threshold or dp[n])

        if n < 0: return False
        if n >= threshold: return True
        return dp[n]

Now, let's write code structure:

import sys, bisect

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it); M = next(it); A = next(it); B = next(it)
    intervals = []
    for _ in range(M):
        L = next(it); R = next(it)
        intervals.append((L,R))
    intervals.sort()
    merged = []
    for L,R in intervals:
        if not merged or L > merged[-1][1] + 1:
            merged.append([L,R])
        else:
            if R > merged[-1][1]:
                merged[-1][1] = R
    intervals = merged
    for L,R in intervals:
        if R - L + 1 >= B:
            print("No")
            return
    segments = []
    cur = 1
    for L,R in intervals:
        if cur <= L-1:
            segments.append((cur, L-1))
        cur = R+1
    segments.append((cur, N))
    K = len(segments) - 1
    starts = [s for s,t in segments]
    entry = [set() for _ in range(K+1)]
    entry[0].add(1)
    # semigroup
    if A == B:
        d = A
        def can(n):
            return n >= 0 and n % d == 0
    else:
        threshold = A * (A - 1)
        dp = [False] * threshold
        if threshold > 0:
            dp[0] = True
            for n in range(1, threshold):
                # iterate d
                for g in range(A, B+1):
                    if g <= n and dp[n-g]:
                        dp[n] = True
                        break
        def can(n):
            if n < 0:
                return False
            if n >= threshold:
                return True
            return dp[n]
    ans = False
    for i, (S,T) in enumerate(segments):
        if not entry[i]:
            if i == K:
                break
            continue
        W = max(S, T - B + 1)
        out = set()
        # local variables for speed
        can_func = can
        entry_set = entry[i]
        for x in range(W, T+1):
            for e in entry_set:
                if e <= x and can_func(x - e):
                    out.add(x)
                    break
        if i == K:
            if N in out:
                ans = True
            break
        if not out:
            continue
        # generate
        for p in out:
            for d in range(A, B+1):
                q = p + d
                if q > N:
                    continue
                j = bisect.bisect_right(starts, q) - 1
                if j < 0:
                    continue
                if q <= segments[j][1]:
                    if j > i:
                        entry[j].add(q)
                    # if j == i, ignore
                # else bad
    print("Yes" if ans else "No")

Need ensure `bisect` imported. Use `from bisect import bisect_right` for speed.

Potential bug: In final segment, if entry[i] nonempty and out computed, if N in out ans True. But what if N is not in out but N was added to entry[i] and entry[i] has N, out includes N because diff0. Good.

Potential bug: If K=0 and entry[0]={1}, process final segment, out computed, ans if N in out. Good.

Potential bug: If entry[i] empty and i==K, break with ans False. But what if N was reached directly from previous segment and added to entry[K], then entry[K] not empty. Good.

Potential bug: If entry[i] empty for a non-final segment, we `continue` without generating. But could there be reachable positions in this segment due to a jump from an earlier segment that landed in last B but not first B? We argued no. Let's test a concrete counterexample: Segment i length 5, B=10, first B covers entire segment, so any landing is in first B. If length >B, direct landing to last B not first B impossible if distance from previous segment >B? Let's test length=15,B=10, previous segment end S-1, q in last B at S+6 (last B starts S+5). Now, let's think about if the DP over segments is complete. We process all possible first landings in each segment (entry) and all possible exits (out). Any path from start to N can be decomposed by the sequence of segments it visits. For each visited segment, the first position in that segment is an entry (landing from previous segment or start). Then within the segment, it may take several jumps before leaving; the last position before leaving is an exit (in last B). The difference from entry to exit is a sum of allowed jumps, so semigroup. Then the jump to next segment is a direct action. Thus our generation captures the transition. By induction, if a path exists, entries/out sets will include its positions. Conversely, any generated entry is reachable via a valid path. So algorithm correct.

Need ensure "first position in a segment" is indeed in entry set. For start segment0, entry {1}. For a later segment, the path enters by a jump from some previous segment's exit p to q. We generate q and add to entry. If the path skips a segment, it doesn't visit it; our DP doesn't need entry there. If the path visits segment i, the first position q in segment i is reached by a jump from some earlier segment h<i. The jump distance <=B. The start p must be in last B of segment h, so p in out_h. We generate q. Good.

Now, let's think about if a path visits a segment but the first position q is not in the first B of that segment. Can that happen? To enter segment i from earlier segment, q is the first position in segment i on the path. Since q is reached by a jump from p in earlier segment, q-p<=B. p < S_i (start of segment i), so q < S_i + B. Thus q is in first B. Good.

Now, let's think about if a path visits a segment and leaves from a position p that is not in last B. To leave to a later segment, q in later segment, q-p<=B. Since q >= S_{i+1} >= T_i+2, p >= T_i+2-B, so p in last B. Good. Thus out captures.

Now, let's think about if there is a path that reaches N and final segment entry nonempty but out doesn't include N because previous segment generation skipped q because q was in a bad interval?
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right


def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it)
    M = next(it)
    A = next(it)
    B = next(it)

    intervals = []
    for _ in range(M):
        L = next(it)
        R = next(it)
        intervals.append((L, R))

    intervals.sort()

    # Merge touching/overlapping bad intervals.
    merged = []
    for L, R in intervals:
        if not merged or L > merged[-1][1] + 1:
            merged.append([L, R])
        else:
            if R > merged[-1][1]:
                merged[-1][1] = R

    intervals = merged

    # A bad interval of length >= B can never be jumped over.
    for L, R in intervals:
        if R - L + 1 >= B:
            print("No")
            return

    # Build safe segments.
    segments = []
    cur = 1
    for L, R in intervals:
        if cur <= L - 1:
            segments.append((cur, L - 1))
        cur = R + 1
    segments.append((cur, N))

    K = len(segments) - 1
    starts = [s for s, _ in segments]

    entry = [set() for _ in range(K + 1)]
    entry[0].add(1)

    # Semigroup generated by all integers in [A, B].
    if A == B:
        d = A

        def can(n):
            return n >= 0 and n % d == 0
    else:
        threshold = A * (A - 1)  # all n >= threshold are representable
        dp = [False] * threshold
        if threshold > 0:
            dp[0] = True
            for n in range(1, threshold):
                for g in range(A, B + 1):
                    if g <= n and dp[n - g]:
                        dp[n] = True
                        break

        def can(n):
            if n < 0:
                return False
            if n >= threshold:
                return True
            return dp[n]

    ans = False

    for i, (S, T) in enumerate(segments):
        if not entry[i]:
            if i == K:
                break
            continue

        # Reachable positions in the last B positions of this segment.
        W = max(S, T - B + 1)
        out = set()
        for x in range(W, T + 1):
            for e in entry[i]:
                if e <= x and can(x - e):
                    out.add(x)
                    break

        if i == K:
            if N in out:
                ans = True
            break

        if not out:
            continue

        # Jump from exit positions to later safe segments.
        for p in out:
            for d in range(A, B + 1):
                q = p + d
                if q > N:
                    continue

                j = bisect_right(starts, q) - 1
                if j < 0:
                    continue

                if q <= segments[j][1]:
                    if j > i:
                        entry[j].add(q)

    print("Yes" if ans else "No")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
